- to count as transparent a pixel with an alpha less than 0xFF00 — a tolerance for rounding: the format says "not fully opaque", and a semi-transparent shadow on dark paper is seen just as badly;
- to decode a picture of any size: the memory is that of the caller's process, and Go has no limit for `image`;
- to warn of a file without a signature: `module.media` already rejects it.

— *Consequence:* `golang.org/x/image` is a direct dependency in `go.mod`, raised by Dependabot `gomod`; `THIRD_PARTY` of the justfile names it, and the release carries `LICENSE-x-image`. — `picture/`; `cmd/module-kit/main.go`, `cmd/module-kit/build.go`; `go.mod`, `go.sum`; justfile; README; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## The commands `index` and `diff`

**R40. The CLI has the commands `index` and `diff` over the library. `index` builds the index of a release of `modules` from the previous index or an explicit `--first`, from the package folders, the tag and the withdrawals as a file or an explicit `--no-withdrawals`, and writes `--out`, which does not exist yet. `diff` compares a new version — a module's folder or a package — with each published version that `--index` names, by their packages from `--published`, or is told `--first` explicitly.**

- **The call of `index`** — `module-kit index (--previous <module-index.json> | --first) --tag vX.Y.Z (--withdrawals <withdrawals.yaml> | --no-withdrawals) --out <file> <package folder>...`. Exactly one flag of each pair. An empty `--previous`, a flag twice or neither of a pair — code 2: a recipe of `modules` to which the shared release did not give the previous index fails, and does not start a new one. The recipe `release-files VERSION PREVIOUS` of `modules` needs only `if [ -z "$PREVIOUS" ]` and a check of whether `withdrawals.yaml` exists.
- **The inputs of `index`** — the package folders that `build` wrote: each is read by `modulepackage.Read` by the chain from the digest of the bytes of its `inventory.json`, with `device/`. The withdrawals — YAML by the rules of the YAML reader, then `$defs/withdrawals` of the index's schema. An empty file of withdrawals is not withdrawals, but a breach of form: "no withdrawals" is said by a flag.
- **The output of `index`** — `--out`, which does not exist yet: the file is written to a temporary folder beside it and moved to its place, as a package by `build`. To stdout — a line with the tag and the number of packages.
- **The codes of `index`** — 0: the index is written; 1: `*moduleindex.Refusal`, a line `<rule ID>: <module> <version>: <words>` per breach and a line of the summary; 2: `ErrPrevious` — including an index of a newer format version —, `ErrRelease`, withdrawals not in their form, a folder that is not a package, input or output.
- **The call of `diff`** — `module-kit diff [--json] (--index <file> | --first) [--published <package folder>]... <module>`. A module that the index does not name is the first version, code 0. `--first` — when there is no index at all yet. The package of each version of the module from the index, withdrawn ones too, is looked for by the `package_digest` of the entry among `--published`; without it — code 2 with the version and the digest. Extra folders are skipped.
- **The new version in `diff`** — a folder with `inventory.json` at the root is a package, `modulepackage.ReadParts` by its digest. With `manifest.yaml` — a module, `module.CheckModule` and `modulepackage.Build`, the solvers one per CPU. Neither — code 2. A module that the check rejected — the report as from `check` and code 1, without a comparison.
- **The report of `diff`** — `Comparison.String()`: the lines `refused: <ID>: <words>`, as the library writes them, and the changes. With `--json` — `Comparison` through `encoding/json`, and for a module that the check rejected — `{"check": [breaches]}`, with the path from the folder of the command line.
- **The flags** — a `flag.FlagSet` of the standard library per command, without the output of the Go package itself: a usage of its own, types of its own for a flag given or not, and for a repeated one.

— *Why:*
- the format text requires that the build be told "no previous one" explicitly, and that an unreadable previous index give a refusal, not a new index; a flag for each "no" makes a missing file an error of the call;
- packages, not modules: the recipe of `modules` builds them anyway — for the archives `sha256-<hex>.tar.gz` of its release, — and the solvers do not run a second time;
- "not in the index — the first version" is needed by the first release of a new module in a repository where others are already published. That the index is of the newest release is held by CI of `modules`, which checks it with `release-verify`. A version below the published ones is also caught by `module-index.version-above` at the index build.

— *Rejected:*
- an empty `--previous` as "no previous one" — so a missing file would silently start a new index;
- module folders at the input of `index` — the solvers would run a second time, and the recipe needs the packages anyway;
- the index to stdout — on a failure half a file would be left in `dist/`;
- to require `--first` for a module that is not in the index too — it breaks the first release of a module beside published ones;
- the text report of `check` with `--json` too — CI that reads JSON would stumble on the parsing;
- a third-party library of flags — a new license in the release for the sake of two commands.

— *Consequence:* `cmd/module-kit/`; README; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## Binaries on each OS

**R41. Each of the six binaries of the release is run in CI on a runner of its own OS, on each pull request and push to `main`. "Binaries / build" builds them with the recipe `release-files`, as the release does, and runs the linux/amd64 binary — its result becomes the reference. "Binaries / \<platform>" on six standard GitHub runners runs `.github/binaries.sh`: the sum by `SHA256SUMS`, `--version`, `check`, `build`, `index`, `diff` and `open` on samples of the format and a byte-for-byte check of what was built against the reference. "Binaries" waits for both and stands in the ruleset `main`.**

- **The runners** — `ubuntu-24.04` (linux/amd64), `ubuntu-24.04-arm`, `macos-15-intel` (darwin/amd64), `macos-15` (darwin/arm64, M1), `windows-2025` (windows/amd64) and `windows-11-arm`: the labels of the table of standard runners for public repositories (sources 3.17).
- **The build** (`ci.yml`, the job `binaries-build`):
  - «As in the pinned .github» — every reference to `.github` is at one SHA, the mount of the Go caches in «Release files and samples» is by the lines of the header of the composite action `toolchain` of that release, and the awk of «The stage of the release» is that of the step «Toolchain stages» of the shared `go.yml`; a move of `.github` that changes either fails here, and the pull request takes it by hand;
  - «The stage of the release» fails the job once the stage `toolchain` of `.devcontainer/Dockerfile` holds more than `toolchain-core`, in which the shared release builds its files: the job builds in `toolchain`, and the two hold the same files only while `toolchain` is `toolchain-core` with its `SHELL` alone;
  - the image of `toolchain` and the Go caches — the composite action `toolchain` of `.github`, by the SHA of its tag, with `check: binaries`, twice: before the build, and at the end of the job with `save: "true"`, so a push to `main` saves the Go caches once the binaries and their reference are built;
  - «Release files and samples» — `docker run` as the runner's user, `HOME=/tmp`, the Go caches mounted at `/go-cache`, `just release-files v0.0.0-ci` and `just binaries-samples`. The recipe `binaries-samples` copies the samples from the Go module `spec` of the version in `go.mod` — CI does not take the submodule — and makes the copies writable: the Go module cache holds them read-only.
- **What the run checks** (`.github/binaries.sh`, bash: Git Bash on Windows, `shasum -a 256` on macOS):
  - the binary's sum is the one in `SHA256SUMS`;
  - `--version` — two lines, `module-kit v…` and `Unicode …`;
  - `check` of the example `with-templates` — code 0: the Starlark sandbox and the solver, and on linux and darwin the mmap of `x/sys`, by which go.starlark.net holds integers;
  - `check` of the vector `task.known-band/unknown-band` — code 1 and the rule's ID in the report;
  - `build` of the vector `packages/with-solver` — its digest: the canonical text and the paths on Windows;
  - `build` of the modules of the vector `indexes/first-release`, `index --first` over their packages and `diff` of one of them — code 0;
  - `index --first` over the packages of `indexes/first-release` and the package of `packages/with-solver`, then `open` over them with the seeds 1 and 2^64 − 1;
  - the files of the built package and of the open views by sha256 and the index are byte for byte the same as those of the linux/amd64 reference: they depend only on the module's files.
- **One required check** — the job "Binaries" with `if: always()`, which fails if the build or even one run is not `success`. It alone stands in the ruleset `main`, as a check of GitHub Actions, not the six names of the matrix, which would change with the labels of the runners.
- **The permissions** — `contents: read` for the whole `ci.yml`, `persist-credentials: false`. The matrix takes only the script from the repository, `sparse-checkout`. The artifact lives 3 days.
- **The actions** — `actions/checkout`, `actions/upload-artifact` and `actions/download-artifact`, pinned by SHA with the version beside it, as in `ci.yml` of `spec`.

— *Why:*
- the binary carries go.starlark.net with `x/sys`, which has code of its own on each OS, and writes files by the paths of the OS: a binary that was never run on its OS may not start;
- the byte-for-byte check against the reference catches what the exit code will not tell: another canonical text or other paths in the package on Windows;
- the image and the Go caches by the action of `.github`: the entry of the image is the one that the jobs of `go.yml` fill and read, and the six cross-builds do not compile with an empty `GOCACHE` on every run;
- the cost is $0: standard GitHub-hosted runners in public repositories are free (sources 3.17).

— *Rejected:*
- a run by hand at the release: a binary that does not start would be found by the author, not by CI;
- six required checks by the names of the matrix — the ruleset would have to be edited with each change of a runner's label;
- a path filter on the job — a skipped required check would hang on a pull request without code, and the minutes are free;
- QEMU on one runner — checks neither macOS nor Windows;
- a full checkout and Go on each runner — the binary is checked as the release will build it, not by a build on the spot.

— *What is not closed:* GitHub may reconfigure or remove the labels `macos-15-intel` and `windows-11-arm`. Then the job will fail with the label's name, and a task will change it.

— *Consequence:* `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; `.github/binaries.sh`; justfile; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## The vector test without a skip list

**R42. The vector test skips not a single case: there is no skip list, and a case that does not pass fails `TestVectors`. So each release passes every vector of the tag of `spec` that it is built on. The releases that others take are of the line that `RELEASE_LINE` of `.github/workflows/release.yml` names: CI of `modules` and `canvas` take the binary of the CLI that `release-verify` accepted, and `api` and the eval runner of `tutor` — the Go module at a tag.**

- **The test** (`internal/vectors`): the sorting into cases, the refusal on a file of a kind that the test does not know, and the refusal on a kind without a single case. Each case is a subtest, and its runner from `runners` must return `nil`.
- **Who takes the release and how** — [architecture.md](architecture.md). CI of `modules` and `canvas` take the binary at an exact tag and check it with `release-verify` before the first run. `api` and the eval runner of `tutor` take the Go module: `go get github.com/altnovatrail/module-kit@<tag>` and `go mod tidy`. The Go module is not a release file: the Go module proxy builds its zip at the tag, and `go.sum` holds its hash, as for the modules of `spec`. The `go.mod` of `module-kit` has no `replace` and `tool` that would hinder the import.

— *Why:* every release passes all the vectors, and the test does not let a skip list grow: while a list and its parsing stood in the code, any pull request could add a line to it, and only the review held this. Without the list a case that does not pass cannot be got around by anything but code, and the test holds the rule. The parsing of a list that does not exist and its helpers would be code that no run executes.

— *Rejected:*
- to keep the file and fail the test when it is not empty: the outcome is the same, but the parsing, the helpers and their tests would remain code that is not executed on an empty list, and "the list must be empty" and "there is no list" are one rule;
- to keep the list for a move to a tag: the new vectors of the tag would stand in it until their code comes, and a release could come out without passing the vectors. A move to a tag that brings vectors without code stays red until the code comes.

— *Consequence:* a move to a new tag of `spec` that brings new vectors brings their code in the same task. — `internal/vectors/`; `.github/workflows/release.yml`; README.md; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## The command `open`

**R43. The command `open` writes the open views of a release of `modules` for the samples page of `canvas`: `module-kit open --index <release index> --latest <newest release index> --seed N [--seed N]... --out <folder> <package folder>...`. For each module it takes the version of a new attempt by the index of the release, with the marks of withdrawal from the newest index. In `open-views.json` each task has an entry with its open view and digest, and a template an instance for each seed. Beside it are the files of the device part in `modules/<ID>/<version>/`. The marks are combined by `moduleindex.WithLatest`, and the open view as a JSON value is given by `openview.TaskView` and `ManifestView`.**

- **The call** (`cmd/module-kit/open.go`):
  - `--index` is the index of the release from which the page is built; `--latest` is the index of the newest release, the same file when the release is the newest. CI of `canvas` has checked both with `release-verify`;
  - `--seed` is a seed of the format: unsigned 64-bit, in decimal digits, with no leading zero. It repeats; the same seed twice, another spelling or no seed at all — usage and code 2. The page build chooses the seeds;
  - the package folders are positional, as with `index` and `diff`, each by the digest of the bytes of its `inventory.json`. An extra folder does no harm, a missing needed one is code 2, and a release with nothing to open needs no folders;
  - `--out` — the folder does not exist or is empty, or else code 2. It is written through a temporary folder beside it, as `build` writes a package.
- **The choice of versions:**
  - `moduleindex.WithLatest(release, latest)` gives the index of the release where each entry is withdrawn if the newest index withdrew it, with its reason. An entry of the release that the newest one lacks, holds otherwise or whose withdrawal it does not hold is `ErrLatest`: such an index is not the newest, and the command exits with code 2;
  - for each module of the release's index, in the order of IDs, — `moduleindex.Newest`: the newest by semver unwithdrawn version within the format's window, as for a new attempt in `api`. Each newer version that it passed over is named on stdout — a withdrawn one with its reason, one of another format version with its number —, and a module without a version by a line `<ID>: no version to open`;
  - the package of the chosen version is read by `modulepackage.Read` by the chain of digests, with `device/`. The ID, the version and the format version of the inventory must be those that the entry names, or else code 2.
- **`open-views.json`** is a canonical text:
  - the root is `format: "module-kit-open-views"`, `version: 0`, `module_format_version` and `modules` in the order of IDs;
  - a module has `id`, `version`, `module_format_version`, `release` — the tag of the entry —, `package_digest`, `manifest` — the open view of the manifest — and `tasks` in the order of task IDs;
  - a task has `{"open_view_digest", "task"}`, and an instance of a template also `seed`, as a string of decimal digits. The instances go in the order of the numbers of the seeds. The instance — the number `template.AttemptInstance(seed, ID, count)` among the `instances` of the package, with the numbers substituted, `template.Instance`, — is the one that an attempt with this seed would get. No solvers run: the package carries the numbers;
  - `task` is the JSON value of the open view before a type took it, `openview.TaskView`, and `open_view_digest` is the digest of exactly its canonical text. So whoever reads the file gets the same digest as the sample has.
- **The files of the device part** — `modules/<ID>/<version>/LICENSE`, `NOTICE` and `media/<path>`, byte for byte those that the chain of digests checked against the listing of the part. The layout is the `device/` of the package: the path of a media file from the open view is found from `media/`, as in the module. The ID is `<publisher>/<name>`, and the pattern of the ID already excludes the names of Windows devices.
- **Codes** — 0 and 2. The command checks no format rules: the packages are read by the chain of digests, so it has no code 1.
- **Tests:**
  - `open_test.go`: `TestOpenPackages` — all the vectors of `packages/`; `TestOpenInstances` — the instances of `missing-factor` by the three seeds of the vector `templates/missing-factor.yaml`; `TestOpenMedia`, `TestOpenWithdrawn`, `TestOpenFails` — the cases of code 2 —, `TestOpenFailsLater`, `TestOpenFormatOfThePackage`, `TestOpenNoModule`, `TestModuleViewsFails`, `TestOpenHelpers`;
  - `moduleindex/latest_test.go`: `TestWithLatest` and `TestWithLatestRefuses` on the vectors of `indexes/`, and `FuzzWithLatest` over a pair of texts through `Read`;
  - `openview`: `TestViews` — the value of `TaskView` and `ManifestView` is equal to the open view of the vectors of `open-view/`, and the digest to the text of that value;
  - `.github/binaries.sh` runs `open` on each OS and checks what it wrote against the linux/amd64 reference.

— *Why:*
- `--seed`: a sample names an instance by the task's ID and the seed (`TaskReference` in `sample.proto` of `spec`). Which seeds the page needs, its build knows; an explicit "at least one" is like the other explicit "none" of the commands;
- a wrapping entry: the element of `canvas` rejects a field that the open view does not have, and the seed and the digest are not fields of a task;
- positional folders — as with `index` and `diff`, and CI of `canvas` names the packages by the entries of the index;
- `modules/<ID>/<version>/` — the same layout as that of `device/`, and the names `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` do not stand beside the media files;
- the value before a type, `TaskView`: the digest is computed from the value before a type, and the text in the file is the same value, so the match is held by code, not by chance. On the vectors of `open-view/task/` and on the examples of tasks the value of the type `open.Task` is the same, but only the agreement of the type generator of `spec` with the marks `x-open` holds this: an empty open object, which the open view keeps, a type with `omitzero` could lose;
- `WithLatest` is in the library: the rule "the newest index knows of all the withdrawals" is a rule of the format, not of the CLI, and `Newest` stays one rule of the choice of a version.

— *Rejected:*
- `--seeds <file>` with seeds by module and task — the form of the file would be invented before `canvas` asked for it;
- one seed without a flag — the page would not show different instances;
- all the instances of a template — up to 10,000 open views per template;
- `seed` and `open_view_digest` right in the object `open.Task` — `canvas` would cut them out before the element;
- `--packages <folder of folders>` — one more form of input beside `index` and `diff`;
- `media/<ID>/<version>/<path>` with `LICENSE` beside it — a layout that is neither in the package nor in the module;
- code 1 for a module without a version — this is not a refusal of the format, but what the release is: stdout names it, and the page is built from the others.

— *Consequence:* `cmd/module-kit/open.go`, `main.go`, `published.go`, `build.go`; `moduleindex/latest.go`; `openview/digest.go`, `openview.go`; `.github/binaries.sh`; README.md; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## The devcontainer image on arm64

**R44. The devcontainer image is built as a whole by a workflow of its own, "Devcontainer", `.github/workflows/devcontainer.yml`: the stage `devcontainer` on `ubuntu-24.04` and `ubuntu-24.04-arm`, as Rebuild Container builds it. Then `.github/devcontainer.sh` checks that the image is of its own architecture and that each tool answers with the version of its `ARG` in the Dockerfile. The workflow runs on pull requests and pushes to `main` that change `.devcontainer/**`, the workflow itself or the script, and by the button. It is not in the ruleset `main`. Д12 and Д14 are closed.**
- **What was done:**
  - the jobs "Devcontainer / amd64" and "Devcontainer / arm64" — `docker build --target devcontainer --tag devcontainer .devcontainer`: the context and the stage are those that `devcontainer.json` names. The build itself checks each archive against its sha256 (R34 spec), and gopls against the lock file `.devcontainer/gopls/` (R19);
  - `.github/devcontainer.sh <image> <Dockerfile> <architecture>` — `dpkg --print-architecture` in the image is the one given. The first line of `node`, `just`, `go`, `buf`, `gh`, `just-lsp`, `claude` and `gopls` in the image names the version of its `ARG`, and not as part of a longer number. `gopls` is in `/usr/local/bin`;
  - the permissions are `contents: read`, `persist-credentials: false`; checkout takes only `.devcontainer` and the script, `sparse-checkout`; the checkout action is the same as in `ci.yml`, v7.0.1 by SHA;
  - on pull request #61 (2026-11-06): "Devcontainer / amd64" — 1 min 18 s, "Devcontainer / arm64" — 1 min 13 s, both green. On arm64 the sums of Node 24.21.0, just 1.58.0, Go 1.27.3, buf 1.75.0, gh 2.101.0 and just-lsp 0.10.0 matched, `go version` is `go1.27.3 linux/arm64`, and gopls was built from the lock file — `golang.org/x/tools/gopls v0.24.0`;
  - the script was checked here before the pull request with a stand-in `docker` that executes the command in this container, built from the same Dockerfile. On amd64 it passed; with the architecture arm64 and with `GO_VERSION=1.27.30` it failed, naming what was wrong. `actionlint` did not run here: running it through `go run` is not allowed in this session. The syntax of the workflow was checked by the run on #61.

— *Why:* the author's decision of 2026-11-06: a workflow of its own by paths, not one run by the button and not a row «в реестр spec». Д12 waited «если CI начнёт собирать образ на arm64», and Д14 waited for Rebuild Container, which checks the gopls step only on the author's machine. Standard runners are free for a public repository (GitHub Docs, "GitHub Actions billing", read 2026-11-05; R41). By paths — because the image changes only with `.devcontainer/`, and a run on each pull request would spend minutes for nothing. Before this, the CI checks built only the stage `toolchain` on amd64: the shared `go.yml` and "Binaries / build".

— *Rejected:*
- one run by the button: the next raise of Go, buf or gopls in the Dockerfile nobody would check on arm64 again;
- a row «в реестр spec», so that CI of `spec` built the image on arm64: the Dockerfile is from the recipe of `spec`, but the gopls step only `module-kit` and `tutor` have (R19; В19), and `spec` would not build it;
- putting "Devcontainer" in the ruleset `main`: a check that the path filter skipped would not report, and a pull request without an edit of `.devcontainer/` would wait for it forever;
- checking the versions inside the Dockerfile: the stage `toolchain` already prints `go version` and `buf --version`, but only `gopls` compares the version with its `ARG`, and the script holds them all by one rule.

— *Consequence:* Д12 and Д14 are closed. This does not replace a rebuild of the image on a Mac with Apple silicon — Docker Desktop and the Dev Containers extension are their own there —, but a wrong arm64 sum is now seen in CI, not by whoever opened the project. The merge gives no release: the workflow and the script are outside the list `paths`. — `.github/workflows/devcontainer.yml`; `.github/devcontainer.sh`; RUN.md, table 4; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.1.

## An anchor with a colon at the end of its name

**R46. An anchor or an alias whose name by YAML 1.2.2 ends with a colon — `&a:`, `*a:` — the YAML reader names a breach of `yaml.no-anchors` by its own pass `colonAnchor` in `internal/yaml/errors.go`, rather than wait for go-yaml. 2SXE and W5VH of the YAML test suite pass, `suiteUnpassed` is empty, Д17 is closed.**
- **Why go-yaml does not read them.** Its scanner of v4.0.0 takes the ASCII characters ns-anchor-char into the name of an anchor, but cuts the trailing colons off on purpose, "to preserve mapping separators immediately after aliases (issue #109)" (`internal/libyaml/scanner.go`, `isAnchorChar` and `scanAnchor`, v4.0.0). yaml/go-yaml#109, "Anchor map key with no space before colon works in v3; breaks in v4", was closed on 2025-11-15 as not planned (read 2026-11-06). So go-yaml reads `&a: key: &a value` as the anchor `a` with a separator and fails at the second colon: "mapping values are not allowed in this context", 1:8 — a reason with no anchor in it. By YAML 1.2.2 the name of the anchor is `a:` (6.9.2), and the file breaks `yaml.no-anchors`.
- **What the pass does.** When go-yaml has refused with an error that `parseError` did not turn into a rule, `colonAnchor` looks at the lines before the place of the refusal, and on the line of the refusal up to its column. It searches for `&` or `*` at the start of a node: first on the line, but for spaces and tabs, after `[`, `{` or `,`, after `-`, `?` or `:` with a space or a tab, and not in a comment. If the name after it by YAML 1.2.2 ends with a colon, this place gets `yaml.no-anchors`, and the message has the words of go-yaml. Otherwise the refusal is "not YAML", as it was.
- **Tests:** `TestRead` — 2SXE and W5VH, and `&` with a colon inside a plain scalar and in a comment — still "not YAML". `TestYAMLTestSuite` passes both cases; without the pass they fail in both tests (a probe). The new functions are covered at 100%, `FuzzRead` — 120 s without findings.

— *Why:* the author's decision of 2026-11-06. The condition of Д17 — «когда go-yaml уберёт 2SXE или W5VH из `known-failing-tests`» — will not come: go-yaml decided not to fix this. The sign of the divergence is narrow and exact: the name by YAML 1.2.2 ends with a colon exactly where go-yaml cuts it. The pass runs only when go-yaml has already refused, so it does not change the verdict on the file — it changes only the reason named, and the module author sees "the format has no anchors", not someone else's markup error.

— *Rejected:*
- accepting it as a known limitation and leaving the cases in `suiteUnpassed`: the module author would see a reason that is silent about the anchor;
- a scanner of anchors of its own, before go-yaml, over the whole file: it would have to repeat the parsing of quotes, block scalars and flow collections, and it is needed only where go-yaml has already refused;
- proposing another reading to go-yaml: #109 was resolved as not planned, and v4 holds this on purpose.

— *Consequence:* the merge will give a release of the line 0.2 — `internal/yaml/errors.go` is in the list `paths`. An amendment to R21. — `internal/yaml/errors.go`, `read_test.go`, `suite_test.go`; RUN.md, table 4; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.6.

## The warning about a number in a text of the whole module

**R50. Of a number in a text of the whole module — a rung template `rung_templates`, a trap advice `trap_advice`, the title and the description of a technique — the module check itself warns: `module.Report` has the field `Warnings`, a warning per text in which the leak check reads at least one number, and the numbers of a text are given by a new function of the package `leak`, `Numbers`. `check` prints them as lines `<path> #/pointer: warning: …` after the breaches, before the warnings about pictures, and the exit code does not change from them. So Д19 is closed.**

- **What is decided** (the author's instruction of 2026-11-08 — «делай Д19» —, the executor's decisions by the format text):
  - **what is warned of** — each text that the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 20, calls a text of the whole module: the rung templates of the manifest, the advice of each trap of the catalog, the title and the description of each technique. The texts of a task — `givens`, `unknown`, the texts of trap values, `simpler_case` — are not: they are for one task, and `task.no-leaks` holds them by the keys of its task. The texts of a lesson are not either: a lesson names its tasks, and `lesson.no-leaks` holds them;
  - **what a number is** — what the leak check reads ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 20, "A number"): `leak.Numbers` gives the numbers of a text by the same steps as the search for a key — `runsIn`, on which `numbersIn` now stands too —, so the warning and `task.no-leaks` never diverge. A number is written as it stands in the normal form of the text: `½` as `1/2`, full-width digits as ASCII, `07:30` as two numbers, `07` and `30`, and "seven", `7th` and `x7` are not numbers. The message names each number once, in the order of the text;
  - **where** — in the package `module`, not as a package of its own, as `picture` is: the texts are parts that the check has already read into the types of `formatv0`, and the warning carries no new third-party module, so `api` takes nothing extra. The warning reads each part whose file passed the schema — in a module that the rules rejected too: the author needs to know of a number in a text in any case. A part that the schema rejected gives no warning, as it gives no breaches of rules either (R23);
  - **order and form** — by the path of the file, then by the place in it, as with breaches; the place is a JSON pointer, without a line and a column, as with the breaches of `task.no-leaks`. The line is `<path> #/pointer: warning: <message>`, and the message repeats why the format wants a text without numbers;
  - **who gets them** — `Check` and `CheckModule`. `CheckFile` does not: it checks the examples and the vectors of parts, and the warning has no vectors. `build` and `diff` print the report of a refusal without the warnings, as they do of pictures (R39);
  - **tests** — `TestNumbers` and a check in `FuzzNumbers`: `Numbers` gives the same numbers that the search reads — each stands in the normal form of the text and has the same value (30 s, about 1.3 million runs, without findings); `TestWarnings` — each kind of text, the order, a rejected module, a part that the schema rejected, the texts of tasks and `CheckFile`; `TestNumbersSaid`; `TestExamplesWarnOfNoNumber` — no example of a manifest, of traps or of techniques of `spec` v0.9.9 holds a number; in the CLI — `TestCheckWarnsOfNumbers`. `TestCheckRefuses` now reads a line of a warning apart: the vectors of `task.no-leaks` `key-in-a-rung-template`, `key-in-a-technique` and `key-in-a-trap-advice` hold a key in a text of the whole module, and that is also a number in it.

— *Why:* the format requires a warning, not a refusal (the 2026-11-08 amendment to R57 spec): `task.no-leaks` finds a number in a text of the whole module only when it is already the key of a task that shows this text — so the epic E3 of `modules` found the numbers in the advice of `wrong-parity` and `time-unit-mixup` only three tasks later. Reading a number by the same code as the search means warning of exactly what may one day become a leak.

— *Rejected:*
- a package of its own, as `picture`: that one stands apart for the sake of the picture decoder, which `api` must not carry (R39), and here there is no new code of dependencies, and the parts have already been read by the check;
- a separate function over `fs.FS`, as `picture.Warnings`: it would read and parse the YAML of all the parts a second time;
- warning of a number in words — "seven", «семь»: the format does not read it as a number ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 20, "What is not read as a number"), and a task whose answer is written so holds it in `leak_phrases`;
- a warning of each number by a separate line: an advice with the multiplication table has eight of them, and the place is one.

— *Consequence:* `leak/*.go` and `module/*.go` are in the list `paths` of the release, so the merge will give a release of the line 0.2. Other repositories need nothing: the field `Warnings` of `Report` is new, and the earlier fields are the same; CI of `modules` will see the warnings in the output of `check`, but the exit code is as before. — `leak/leak.go`, `leak/doc.go`, their tests; `module/warnings.go`, `module/check.go`, `module/doc.go`, their tests; `cmd/module-kit/main.go` and tests; README.md; RUN.md, the start of table 4; [architecture.md](architecture.md), the status, sections 1, 3 and 3.1.

## The tag of `spec` v0.12.0 and format 0.4

**R52. `module-kit` is on `spec` v0.12.0: the submodule `spec/` is at this tag, commit 5b4640a, and the Go modules `spec` and `spec/contracts` are v0.12.0 too, the tag of the nested module is `contracts/v0.12.0`. The releases move to the line 0.3: the first is v0.3.0. So Д18 is closed.**
- **What the tags bring** (`git diff v0.9.16 v0.12.0` in `spec/`, read 2026-11-09). v0.10.0 is the epic E6 of `spec`, format 0.4 ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 2; R108 spec, R111 spec; Д108, Д121 and Д135 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`; row 3.8 of COORDINATION.md of `spec`); v0.10.1, v0.11.0 and v0.12.0 are the archive of E6, the format of the deployment configuration `deployment/` and an edit of the admission `admission/`: `module-kit` does not read them. RULES.md and `.devcontainer/` of `spec` are the same; in the template of the kind `go` a comment of the Dockerfile changed;
- **what was done in the code:**
  - **the window of versions** — `module.FormatVersion` is `0.4`, and `ReadsFormat` accepts only it ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 2). The copy of the schemas `internal/schema/schemas/` is v0.12.0, with two new ones: `page.schema.json` and `page-vector.schema.json` (R23);
  - **`yaml.printable`** — a character outside the printable set of YAML 1.2.2, wherever it stands, in quotes too, the reader rejected before as well, but without an ID, as "not YAML" (R45 spec); now the refusal names the rule ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 4);
  - **template expressions** begin with a token and are nested no deeper than 64 levels — a parenthesis, a unary `-` and `not`. This is now a rule of the subset, in `typeOf`: the dialect parses every expression of the subset, and the check against go.starlark.net stayed as a second line: an expression of the subset that the dialect still does not parse is a breach, not a panic of `compiled` ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 23; В28 — R102 spec; R32);
  - **the kind `time`** — `normal.Time` and `normal.TimeNotation` read a time by the steps 1–5 of a number, `H:MM` or `HH:MM` with minutes of two digits, or of three that begin with no zero, and `normal.Clock` compares the hours and the minutes as numbers, without a sum of minutes: `5:60` is not `6:00`. The verdict `timeVerdict`, the traps, `NormalForm` and the join of two readings — by the same. The leak check finds a key of a time where a time of the text has the same hours and minutes, and a time of a text is a run of digits, `:` and digits in the notation of a time with no character of a word at its edges ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), sections 15 and 20; R111 spec);
  - **`notation: word`** of a choice written by hand — `normal.ChoiceOf` (in `internal/normal/choice.go`) also drops `.`, `!`, `?` and spaces at the end, and one word of letters that is not among the options is `not_yet`, and `normal.Word` recognizes it: a letter of the category L, then letters and marks M. `task.choice-key` requires of the key one word, its end as written, and `task.choice-options` no option that normalization would leave empty ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 15);
  - **the picture paths of a template** hold placeholders: an instance substitutes the numbers into all the strings, the path too, `task.known-pictures` now checks the path of each instance, and in a task that is not a template a brace in a path is a breach of `task.placeholders`, as is a placeholder of a solver's result in any path, in the path of a picture of the solution too: `takesResults` lets a result only into the text of the solution. The picture path of an instance is among the texts of `task.no-leaks`, the path of a task without a template is not ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), sections 20 and 23);
  - **the leak check** also searches the leading question `leading_question` — in the file of the task —, the title and the description of each solving step — a text of the whole module, for each task —, the texts of the numbered list of a lesson, and in a worked example on a workbook the leak phrases in the texts of its lines, but not the keys, and the line of the example that solves a line of the task: the same pieces in the same order, the texts in the normal form, the value of each box equal to the key, as the check of the box compares it. The normal form of the search is `normal.Search`: `leak` searches by it, the module check compares the texts of lines by it too, and `leak` gained no exported name ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 20). The check now reads the solving steps as a part of their own, `parts.steps`, and warns about a number in them, as about a number in a text of the whole module (R50);
  - **the workbook page** — the package `page`: `Lay` lays out the item lines by the numbers and the width table of `page.yaml` ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 14) and gives the places of the number, the texts and the boxes, or `*NoRoom` with the item whose row has no room; `TextWidth` and `Fits` give the width of a text and whether a value fits in a box. A copy of `page.yaml` is embedded in the package and held by a test byte for byte against `spec.Format`, as the copy of the schemas is: the package does not import `spec`, and a consumer of the library carries one file, not all the files of the format. On it stand `task.workbook-page`, on a task without a template and on each instance, and `lesson.workbook-page` with `lesson.example-fills` — in `module/workbook.go`. The message about a lack of room is one: with the numbers of `page.yaml` the rows end at about the twentieth item, long before `most_items`, 99;
  - **version comparison** — for a choice the meaning is held by `notation` too — `options` without it —, and by the options in the normal form of that notation; for a time, by the key and the traps by hours and minutes. The leading question and the description of a step are a patch by the general rule "anything else" ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 27);
  - **the index build** already refused a previous index of a newer format version and one that the schema of its own version does not accept (`ErrPrevious`, R35) — what 0.4 wrote into the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 26; no edit was needed;
  - **the vector test** knows the kind `pages/` — `pages_test.go` —, the verdict kind `time`, `page.yaml` as a file that is no case, and `page-vector.schema.json` of the page vectors. The vectors of `versions/` of the tag — packages of previous format versions by places — the code passed without edits. `TestDigestsRefuse` (R51) — without changes;
  - **tests** — new ones: `normal` — the time and the notation `word`; `leak` — the key of a time; `verdict` — a time without a key and with a key not in notation; `module` — `solves`, the search for phrases in the lines of an example, an empty option of a choice `word`, a lack of room and a value too wide; `moduleversion` — the meaning of the notation and of the time; `page` — 99.7% of the lines, a fuzz test, a check against the layout of `spec` `tools/format-check/page.mjs` on 20,000 random tasks without divergences, by a one-time probe. The tests that called 0.3 the current version are shifted by one version: the current one is 0.4, the previous 0.3, the future 0.5;
- **the release** — `RELEASE_LINE` is `0.3`, and the merge will give v0.3.0. The line 0.2, v0.2.0–v0.2.7, reads format 0.3, and the line 0.3 reads 0.4; `page/page.yaml` is in the list `paths`, since the binary embeds it (R18);
- **the container** — the Dockerfile from the template's generator `tools/new-repo/` for the kind `go` at v0.12.0, into a temporary folder, as in R51: only the comment of the gopls step changed, and it says itself that the copy is held only by the repository's own check — in `module-kit` this is the check of `ci-lint`;
- **checked:** `gh release verify v0.12.0 -R altnovatrail/spec` — "Release v0.12.0 verified!". `ci-lint`, `ci-test` — all the cases of `spec.Format` and `contracts.Vectors` of the tag without skips —, `ci-vuln`, `ci-licenses` with the list of licenses from `go.yml` of `.github` and `just -f spec/justfile docs-check-repo` are green.

— *Why:* the author's instruction of 2026-11-09 — to move to the latest version of `spec`, without an epic and a task of its own, and to bring it to the merge. Row 3.8 of COORDINATION.md of `spec` names exactly this: format 0.4 in the code, the window on 0.4 and a release of its own —, and `modules` moves to 0.4 only after a release of `module-kit` with it (row 3.9). A new line: in versions 0.y of semver an incompatible change is carried by the minor number, and a release that rejects every module of 0.3 is an incompatible change for `modules`, `canvas`, `api` and the eval runner of `tutor`.

— *Rejected:*

- taking `page.yaml` from `spec.Format` at run time: the root of the module `spec` embeds all the files of the format, and `api` would carry them (R23);
- a window on 0.3 and 0.4 at once: while the format is v0, the platform reads only the current version ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 2).

— *Consequence:* the merge will give the release v0.3.0, the first of the line 0.3, — row 3.9 of COORDINATION.md of `spec` waits for it: `modules` moves the modules to 0.4, and CI of `modules` and `canvas` to the binary v0.3.0, having checked it with `release-verify`; `api` and the eval runner of `tutor` take the Go module at the tag v0.3.0. ## The token savings of sessions

**R55. `module-kit` takes the order of work and the structure of the documents of R143 spec and R145 spec by two tasks. T43 — `.claude/settings.json` by the template of the kind `go`, the rules of parts and a thin CLAUDE.md, the rules of RUN.md by the template and `DEBT.md`, the sources and the measurements in files of their own, the budgets of size. T44 — the translation of `docs/` into English. Beyond the template — one case of rule 9 of RUN.md: when there is no current epic, the body of a done task outside an epic goes to the archive of the last done epic. Until T44, rule 10 of RUN.md and Documents in CLAUDE.md also differ from the template: `docs/` are still in Russian.**
- **What was taken** (T43; row 7.1 of COORDINATION.md spec; T123 spec):
  - `.claude/settings.json` — byte for byte what `tools/new-repo/` of `spec` v0.16.3 builds for the kind `go`. From the template's file — `claudeMdExcludes` with the line of Д215 spec, the compaction at 400 thousand, the claude.ai connectors turned off and the hook `UserPromptSubmit` from `spec/tools/session/`; from the kind `go` — the marketplace `novatrail` in `./spec/claude-plugins` and `gopls-lsp@novatrail` (R143 spec and its 2026-11-12 amendment). The plugin runs gopls from `PATH` — the one that the stage `devcontainer` builds from the lock file `.devcontainer/gopls/` (R19, R51). The file was built by the function `addClaudeSettings` from `spec/tools/new-repo/claude.mjs` in a temporary folder, and `checkClaudePlugins` from the same place found the marketplace and the plugin;
  - the rules of parts: `.claude/rules/go.md` — the Go code, `go.mod`, `go.sum` and the embedded copies of the format's files, with the checks `ci-lint`, `ci-test`, `ci-vuln` and `ci-licenses`, — and `.claude/rules/ci.md` — CI, the release and the devcontainer. Before, the checks of the parts were only in [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.1, and in the comments of the justfile, and a rule gives them to a session as soon as the session opens a file of the part. CLAUDE.md names both rules, and its Environment also the documents check with budgets and `.claude/settings.json`;
  - RUN.md — the rules «Как пользоваться» and «Как устроен план: эпики» of the template v0.16.3. This is one task — one session; the body of a done task goes to the archive at once; table 4 is in [DEBT.md](../run/DEBT.md); when an epic is archived, a measurement of the tokens spent (R145 spec). There were no bodies of done tasks in RUN.md: E1 and E2 are already in the archive;
  - "Sources" — the 47 items of [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 4. 44 of them became records of [sources.md](sources.md), under six topics, and the 3 measurements — of the sandbox, of the YAML readers and of the size of `spec.Format` — became [measurements.md](measurements.md), section 1. The items moved word for word, and each record begins with the decision or the section it was read for; the results of the measurement of the sandbox, for which the item referred to R11, are copied into measurements 1.1. The claims that cited architecture 4 and the external facts of architecture 3.1 and 3.6 cite the records, and the numbers of the measurement in architecture 3.4 were replaced by a reference to measurements 1.1. [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 4, became a pointer: the log and the archives refer to it, and they are not rewritten (the 2026-11-12 amendment to R143 spec);
  - [docs-budgets.json](../docs-budgets.json) — the limit of the section architecture 3.6, 84 KB of 1,024 bytes, over the block `repository`: in Russian the section takes 76,445 bytes, ≈ 74.7 KB, and the limit is the size plus 10–20%, as in measurements 1.9 spec. T44 will lower or remove the limit, by the size of the translation (R145 spec).
- **The split.** The translation is ≈ 835 KB, more than one check (rule 11 of RUN.md). `translation-check <ref>` checks the translation against the commit before it, so the restructuring goes as a task and a commit of its own, and the whole of `docs/` is translated by one task, T44, as T138 spec translated 924 KB by one task.
- **Without a current epic.** Rule 9 of the template sends the body of a task outside an epic to the archive of the current epic, and `module-kit` has none: E2 is done, and the next one is not chosen. The body goes to the archive of the last done epic, to the section «Задачи вне эпика, сделанные после него» — now this is [plan/E2.md](plan/E2.md). The gist sentence stands at the start of «Задачи вне эпика» of RUN.md and leaves it together with the next epic. So says rule 9 of RUN.md, and `spec` gets the question as row В35 of RUN.md.

— *Why:* T123 spec gives each repository one task outside an epic, and a session whose task is more than one check proposes to split it. On 2026-11-12 the author chose two tasks and the archive of E2. Without the case "no epic" the body of T43 would stay in RUN.md, against R143 spec, and an archive file of its own would have to be remembered by the search for a task by its heading.

- the restructuring and the translation by one task, with the check against `git stash create`, as in T136 spec: in the review, the diff of the translation would hide the edit of the restructuring;
- an epic E3 of T43 and T44: T123 spec sets a task outside an epic;
- the bodies wait in RUN.md for the next epic: against "the body of a done task goes to the epic's archive at once" of R143 spec. An archive file of its own for tasks outside an epic was rejected by the 2026-11-12 amendment to R143 spec;
- `sources.md` in English at once: that would be a translation without a check, since `translation-check` has no original. As in `spec`, the records move in Russian, and T44 translates them (T133 spec, T137 spec);
- one rule `go.md`, without `ci.md`: the checks of CI and of the release would stay scattered over architecture 3.1 and the justfile.

## The short RUN.md

**R56. RUN.md keeps only what a session acts on and what is not said elsewhere, and has no tasks outside an epic. A task that does not come from an epic — a move to a tag of `spec`, an analysis by R145 spec — goes into table 1 of the current epic, and when there is none, it is the first task of the next. The rules keep their numbers, so that the citations of the log and the archives hold.**
- **What was cut** (2026-11-13, at the author's request to save tokens): the section «Задачи вне эпика» with the gists of T43 and T44 — their bodies are in [plan/E2.md](plan/E2.md), and the line of E2 in «Сделанные эпики» names them; the second item of rule 9 — where the body of a task outside an epic goes; the history of E1 and E2 at the start of the file, which «Сделанные эпики» says; «Ссылки», which RULES.md, Citations, says; the text of rules 3 and 5, which RULES.md, Hard rules and How we work, say, and the list of what `docs-check-repo` checks in rule 6; the empty table 2 and the long openings of tables 1–3. The statuses, «Кто», «План» and the body of a task are each one paragraph. RUN.md went from 23,044 bytes to 10,680.
- **В35.** The question to `spec` was where the body of a done task outside an epic goes when there is no current epic, and its recommendation was what `module-kit` did (R55). With no tasks outside an epic the question is no longer `module-kit`'s: the row left table 3 with this answer of the author, and the template of `spec` keeps its own rule 9.

— *Why:* RUN.md is read by every task's session, so each byte of it is paid for in every session (R143 spec, R145 spec). What CLAUDE.md and RULES.md say is loaded into each session anyway, and the history of epics is in their archives.

— *Rejected:*
- to renumber the rules after cutting 3 and 5: R55 and the archives cite rules 9, 10 and 11 by number;
- to keep «Задачи вне эпика» empty, as in the template: one more rule and section for a case the author does not need;
- to keep В35 for `spec`: `module-kit` would ask about a case it no longer has.

— *Consequence:* RUN.md; the 2026-11-13 amendment to R55.

— *Amendment 2026-11-16 (R57):* RUN.md and the tables of `run/` are now in English, by R154 spec; the rules keep their numbers, and rules 3 and 5 stay a line with a pointer, as above. There is still no task outside an epic, and no `run/TASKS.md` of R155 spec: the template keeps the rule of a repository whose own rule makes such a task a task of an epic (the 2026-11-13 amendment to R19 spec). A task the author starts between epics is the first task of the next epic and starts it: so T45 started E3. The list of done epics left RUN.md for [plan/E3.md](plan/E3.md), section «Из RUN.md и DEBT.md, при переходе на `run/`» (R154 spec): an archive is found by its file, `docs/plan/<epic>.md`, and T43 and T44 by the search of a task by its heading.

There is no `run/TASKS.md`, against R155 spec, by R56. ## The shared checks of `.github` v0.1.24

**R58. CI of `module-kit` calls the shared checks of `.github` at v0.1.24, its latest release, by the SHA of its tag, fab102d, in every `uses:` of `.github/workflows/`; its own job "Binaries / build" takes the image of the stage `toolchain` from the cache of GitHub Actions by the steps of the shared `go.yml` — the same file, path and key, so one entry serves both —, and Dependabot raises every reference to `.github` in one pull request, the group `shared-workflows`. The input `same-bits` stays off. Epic E3 is done.** T46 of epic E3 (R57); Д146 spec and Д88 spec; as T68 `sim` (R63 sim).
- **What v0.1.24 brings** against v0.1.15 (sources 3.12):
  - each job of `go.yml` and `documents.yml` takes the image of `toolchain` from the caller's cache, by the key `toolchain-<OS>-<architecture>-<sha256 .devcontainer/Dockerfile>`, and builds it only on a miss (R36 .github; the 2026-11-09 amendment to R31 spec); `documents.yml` does it through `TOOLCHAIN_IMAGE_FILE` of `spec`'s `toolchain.sh`, which `spec` v0.18.3 has;
  - `go.yml` has one new input, `same-bits`, `false` by default, and without it the jobs are those of v0.1.15: the four recipes of `.claude/rules/go.md` and the coverage, with the same `ALLOWED_LICENSES`;
  - `release.yml` — no input changes, a longer timeout and the wait for an npm package, which `module-kit` does not publish; `codeql.yml` and `scorecard.yml` — `github/codeql-action` v4.39.3; `secrets.yml` and `dco.yml` — unchanged. `release-verify` of `spec` v0.18.3 takes a signature of the shared `release.yml` at any release of `.github` from v0.1.7, so v0.1.24 too.
- **What is changed here:**
  - `.github/workflows/` — the seven references, from `89d3cee # v0.1.15` to `fab102d # v0.1.24`, in `ci.yml`, `codeql.yml`, `release.yml` and `scorecard.yml`;
  - `ci.yml`, the job "Binaries / build" — the steps «Toolchain image from the cache», «Toolchain image» and «Toolchain image into the cache» of `go.yml` v0.1.24, byte for byte, in place of `docker build`. The image keeps the tag `toolchain`, which the step «Release files and samples» runs. Before them, the step «Cache steps as in the shared go.yml» reads `go.yml` at the SHA that the job "Go" pins, by `gh api`, and fails the job if its first copy of the three steps is not the job's, or is not there;
  - `.github/dependabot.yml` — the group `shared-workflows`, `patterns: ["altnovatrail/.github/*"]`, under `github-actions`, by the template of architecture 3.3 .github;
  - [.claude/rules/ci.md](../.claude/rules/ci.md) and [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.1 — the version, the cache, and the version of the shared `codeql.yml`, which section 3.1 still gave as v0.1.10.
- **Not changed:** `devcontainer.yml` builds the stage `devcontainer` from the network on purpose: what it checks is that the image builds, on amd64 and arm64, with the tools at their `ARG` (R44), and a cache would hide a broken download.

— *Why:* while CI is on v0.1.15, each job builds the image from the network, and an outage of nodejs.org, GitHub or the snapshot of the Ubuntu archive fails the checks (Д146 spec). "Binaries / build" is one such job, so it takes the cache too. One entry for it and the jobs of `go.yml`: the same Dockerfile, the same command, the same image `toolchain` and the same runner, `ubuntu-24.04`, and an entry of an image is about 575 MB of the 10 GB of a repository (the 2026-11-09 amendment to R31 spec). One pull request for all the references to `.github`, since they name one release (Д88 spec). `same-bits` is the option of R107 spec for the two libraries whose bits must be the same on every build, `learner-model` and `engine` (R09 learner-model; R10 engine); R107 spec does not name `module-kit`, and "Binaries" already holds what its binaries build to the byte on six systems (R41).

— *Rejected:*
- "Binaries / build" building its image on every run — the risk of Д146 spec stays for the one required check of the binaries;
- a path or key of its own — a second entry of the same image in the cache. The copy of the steps has its price: Dependabot moves the SHA of `go.yml`, not the copy, so a pull request that moves `.github` holds the steps to its `go.yml` by hand, as [.claude/rules/ci.md](../.claude/rules/ci.md) says, and the step «Cache steps as in the shared go.yml» fails "Binaries", a required check, until it does; `.github` has no shared action for the steps alone;
- the copy without that step — a grouped pull request of Dependabot would go green with the old steps, and on a merge by green checks nobody would see it (`/code-review high` of T46);
- `same-bits: true` — four jobs more on every pull request for a rule that no decision gives `module-kit`; taking it would be the author's decision, not a step of this task;
- the cache in `devcontainer.yml` — above;
- waiting for Dependabot — it would raise each reference in a pull request of its own, and not the steps of "Binaries / build".

— *Consequence:* the 2026-11-16 amendments to R06, R07, R18, R41 and R57. The merge changes `release.yml`, which is in the list `paths` (R18), and so gives the next release of the line 0.3, v0.3.4 if none comes before it, of the same code as v0.3.3 — the binaries differ by the version that `release-files` writes into them —, signed by the shared `release.yml` v0.1.24. The first run on `main` fills the cache of `main`; until then a pull request misses and builds the image. Epic E3 is in its archive, [plan/E3.md](plan/E3.md), with the measurement of its sessions in measurements 2.1; table 1 has no current epic, and the next is the author's choice from `run/EPICS.md`. — `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `codeql.yml`, `release.yml`, `scorecard.yml`; `.github/dependabot.yml`; `.claude/rules/ci.md`; RUN.md; [plan/E3.md](plan/E3.md); [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.1; [sources.md](sources.md), record 3.12; [measurements.md](measurements.md), section 2.1. — RUN.md, T46.

## The speed of CI, row 7.18 spec

**R59. CI of `module-kit` is made faster by what row 7.18 of `COORDINATION.md` spec names and does not wait for: `release-files` runs its six cross-builds at once; `TestVectors` runs its cases as parallel subtests, but for the verdicts of a predicate, `verdicts/predicate/`, which run one at a time before the rest; `TestChecksTheFormat` checks each module and each file of a part in a parallel subtest of its own. What the checks prove is the same. The rest of the row, the move to the release of `.github` of row 7.13, is T48, and the move to the tag of `spec` of row 7.14 with the stage `toolchain-core`, which changes `.devcontainer/`, is T49, the last task of epic E4.** T47 of epic E4; row 7.18 of `COORDINATION.md` spec, the sixth 2026-11-15 amendment to R76 spec and R156 spec; measurements 3.1 spec: "`module-kit` | 2.3–2.7 min | Go / test, 122 s | `-race -covermode=atomic` without parallel tests; 6 cross-builds one after another in Binaries".
- **The builds.** Each `go build` of `release-files` is a process of its own in the background, over the one build cache, which Go shares between processes; the recipe waits for each and fails after all six have ended, printing the log of a build that failed under its platform — each build writes to a log of its own, outside `dist/` —, and a recipe that stops before the builds end stops them by its `trap`. A build compiles its own packages for its own target, so the six do not wait on each other, and the binaries are those that one build after another gives: `SHA256SUMS` was the same byte for byte (measurements 3.1). On the four cores of a runner of CI the step that runs the recipe took 47 s and 70 s, against 44–72 s before: no gain that the spread of runs shows, since one `go build` already keeps four cores busy; the recipe keeps the builds at once, which row 7.18 names, for a runner of more cores and for a build by hand: on twenty cores of a busy host they took 33 s against 69–93 s, two runs each, too few to count as a gain (measurements 3.1).
- **The tests.** Every case of `TestVectors` shares nothing but the read-only file systems `spec.Format` and `contracts.Vectors` and the tables of the test, and every module and file of `TestChecksTheFormat` nothing but `spec.Format`, so each is a subtest with `t.Parallel()`; the race detector, which `ci-test` runs, would find what they shared. The verdicts of a predicate, the cases of `format/vectors/verdicts/predicate/`, stay one at a time: two of them, `steps-below-the-limit` and `steps-reach-the-limit`, take a predicate to its million steps, which under `-race` takes a good part of the clock of a second that `sandbox.Predicate` gives it (R11), and with the other cases running beside them the first one was stopped by the clock, "unchecked" where the vector gives "correct" (measurements 3.1). A parallel subtest waits until its parent's function has returned, so these cases, run in the loop without `t.Parallel()`, have no other case of their package beside them. Other packages still run beside them, each in a process of its own, as before, and `module` now with its parallel subtests: with them beside it on four cores, `steps-below-the-limit` took 0.31–0.42 s of the second (measurements 3.1). The margin on a runner of CI is not measured, and it is Д20. In CI, `internal/vectors` took 22.1–22.2 s against 28.8–44.2 s before, and `module` 37.5–37.9 s against 40.4–57.2 s; `ci-test` 65–66 s against 65–95 s, since it also compiles the module under `-race` and waits for `FuzzCheck` of `module`, a test of one goroutine (measurements 3.1).
- **What waits** (step 2 of T47): the move to the release of `.github` of row 7.13 — the cache of Go as a volume of the container, one file of the image `toolchain`, the release's checks in parallel — is T48, and "Binaries / build" takes the new steps with it, as R58 says; the move to the tag of `spec` of row 7.14, whose stage `toolchain-core` the release of row 7.13 runs its checks without browsers and Go tests in, is T49, the last, since it changes `.devcontainer/` and the author rebuilds the container after its merge.

— *Why:* the row is the author's word of 2026-11-16 that CI goes faster now, in the run of the orchestrator T171 spec, and only what changes no proof: the same cases, the same binaries, `-race` and the coverage as before. On the four cores of a standard runner, `go test ./...` already runs the packages of the module side by side, and what is left is the tail of the two longest packages, `module` and `internal/vectors`, each a test of one goroutine.

— *Rejected:*
- a longer clock for the predicates in the test — the test would pass the vectors with limits other than those the library runs with (R11);
- `ci-test` without `-race`, or a run of the vectors outside it — what the check proves would change, and row 7.18 does not name it;
- `t.Parallel()` in `FuzzCheck` and in the other tests of `module` — not in the row; FuzzCheck runs its corpus, 12 s under `-race` with the coverage on four cores, and is a candidate for a row of its own;
- the kind `verdicts` whole one at a time — its other cases, of `choice`, `number`, `fraction` and the rest, run no predicate, and the clock is what the exception is for;
- the move to `.github` of row 7.13 now — its release is not out, and the task waits for no release (step 1 of T47).

— *Consequence:* the 2026-11-16 amendments to R07, R20 and R22; Д20 in `run/DEBT.md`, the margin of the predicates at the clock. The `justfile` is in the list `paths` (R18), so the merge gives the next release of the line 0.3, of the same code. — `justfile` (`release-files`); `internal/vectors/vectors_test.go`; `module/format_test.go`; RUN.md, T48 and T49; [plan/E4.md](plan/E4.md); [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1 and 3.6; [measurements.md](measurements.md), section 3.1. — RUN.md, T47.

— *Amendment 2026-11-16 (R60):* in `ci-test` the verdicts of a predicate run in a `go test` of their own after the rest, with nothing beside them, and print their times into the log of "Go / test"; so do the packages `internal/sandbox` and `verdict`, whose tests also take Starlark to the clock — `TestConcurrentEval` was stopped by it beside the other packages. Д20 is closed and deleted from `run/DEBT.md`.

— *Amendment 2026-11-16 (R61):* T48 is done: CI is on `.github` v0.1.30, the release of row 7.13 but for T66 `.github`, and "Binaries / build" takes the caches of Go with the image. What T66 gives — the stage `toolchain-core` — T49 takes, with the tag of `spec` of row 7.14.

— *Amendment 2026-11-16 (R62):* T49 is done: `module-kit` is on `spec` v0.18.4 with the stage `toolchain-core`, and CI on `.github` v0.1.31; epic E4 is done.

## The tests at the clock of a predicate apart

**R60. `ci-test` runs the tests that take Starlark to the clock of a second of `sandbox.Predicate` after the rest of the module's tests, with nothing beside them: first the module but for them, then the packages `internal/sandbox` and `verdict` one at a time, `-p 1`, then the verdicts of a predicate of `TestVectors`, `format/vectors/verdicts/predicate/`, alone and with `-v`, so that the log prints each case with its time. The three profiles are one `coverage/go.out`. The recipe fails if the last run ran no case. What the check proves is the same — the same tests, under `-race`, with the same coverage —, and a plain `go test ./...` runs them as before. This closes Д20.** T50 of epic E4, the night's work on the author's word of 2026-11-16 in the run of the orchestrator T171 spec (R156 spec): T48 and T49 wait for the release of `.github` of row 7.13 and the tag of `spec` of row 7.14, and Д20 of T47 closes inside the repository; a task outside the epic's row stands in table 1 of the current epic (R56).
- **What is at the clock.** Under `-race` a Starlark step is several times slower, and the clock of a predicate, 1 s, is the library's (R11). Before, on four cores of a busy host, `steps-below-the-limit.yaml` took up to 0.87 s and `TestConcurrentEval` — 32 evaluations at once — up to 0.82 s; with the predicate cases alone after the rest, `TestConcurrentEval`, still beside the other packages, was stopped by the clock in 1.17 s (measurements 3.2). The tests of `internal/sandbox` and `verdict` take a predicate to its million steps too, `TestCompile` and "the limit of steps", so the packages go out whole: their tests share the package's process, and naming each test would miss the next one written. No other package evaluates a predicate near its steps.
- **How.** `CLOCK_PACKAGES` and `PREDICATE_CASES` are variables of the `justfile`: the first run is `go list ./...` without the first, with `-skip` by the second; an element of a `-run` pattern is an element of a test's name, and a case's name is its path in `spec`. A folder that `spec` renames would leave the last run without cases, and the recipe then fails rather than drop them. The later profiles join the first without their mode lines, as `go test` joins those of its packages: a block covered twice is twice in it, as with `-coverpkg=./...` already (R20). After, alone on four cores, `steps-below-the-limit.yaml` took 0.26–0.32 s and `TestConcurrentEval` 0.23–0.39 s, and the two later runs 4 s and 2 s (measurements 3.2).

— *Why:* Д20 named the risk — "unchecked" where the vector gives "correct", at random on a slower or busier runner — and two ways back, a test of its own after the others or `-p` lower. The first takes the contention away from the tests at the clock alone and keeps the parallel packages of R59 for the rest. On a runner of CI the cases at the clock then took 0.15 s and 0.12 s of the second, and the step `ci-test` 71 s against 65–66 s (measurements 3.2).

— *Rejected:*
- `ci-test` with `-p` lower — every package waits for the others, and with any `-p` above 1 the tests at the clock still have a package beside them;
- a longer clock in the tests — R59 rejected it: the tests would pass with limits other than those the library runs with (R11);
- only the verdicts of a predicate apart, as Д20 named — `TestConcurrentEval` then failed by the clock beside the other packages (measurements 3.2);
- `-v` for `internal/sandbox` and `verdict` too — 556 lines in the log for two tests whose times measurements 3.2 holds;
- waiting until CI fails — the local runs measured 0.87 s of the second before, and a failure at night stops the orchestrator's merge.

— *Consequence:* the 2026-11-16 amendments to R18, R20 and R59; Д20 is deleted from `run/DEBT.md`. The `justfile` is in the list `paths` (R18), so the merge gives the next release of the line 0.3, of the same code. — `justfile` (`ci-test`); `internal/vectors/vectors_test.go`; [.claude/rules/go.md](../.claude/rules/go.md); RUN.md, T50; [plan/E4.md](plan/E4.md); [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1 and 3.6; [measurements.md](measurements.md), section 3.2. — RUN.md, T50.

## The shared checks of `.github` v0.1.30

**R61. CI of `module-kit` calls the shared checks of `.github` at v0.1.30, the release of row 7.13 of `COORDINATION.md` spec but for its last task, by the SHA of its tag, 8311cbf, in every `uses:` of `.github/workflows/`; "Binaries / build" takes the image of the stage `toolchain` and the caches of Go by a copy of the steps of the job `arm64` of the shared `go.yml` — the one job there that takes the image on its own —, with the check `binaries` in the key of the Go caches, and the step «Cache steps as in the shared go.yml» holds the copy, comments and blank lines aside, and the mount of the Go caches to that job at the SHA that the job "Go" pins. What the checks prove is the same.** T48 of epic E4; row 7.13 and row 7.18 of `COORDINATION.md` spec, the sixth 2026-11-15 amendment to R76 spec and R156 spec; measurements 3.1 spec.
- **What v0.1.30 brings** against v0.1.24 (sources 3.13): the caches of Go — the modules and the builds — from the caller's cache of GitHub Actions, one folder mounted at `/go-cache` in every `docker run`, by a key of the check, checked by `go mod verify` before the work, saved by a push to `main` alone (R41 .github); one file of the image `toolchain`, `novatrail-toolchain.tar` of the image `novatrail-toolchain`, the file of `spec`'s `toolchain.sh`, in `go.yml` and `documents.yml` alike, built at most once a run by the first job of `go.yml`, `toolchain`, which hands it on by an artifact, and saved by a push to `main` alone (R42 .github); with `same-bits` alone, the targets as a matrix and `fma-diff` at once (R43 .github); `release.yml` signs and checks its files eight at once and holds the published ones to the release's attestation by one call (R44 .github); `codeql.yml` takes Go from the archive of go.dev by `ARG GO_VERSION` and `GO_SHA256_AMD64` of the stage `toolchain`, without building it (R45 .github), which this repository's Dockerfile has as `spec`'s. No input of the workflows it calls changed, and no workflow of v0.1.30 runs in a stage `toolchain-core`, which `.devcontainer/Dockerfile` has not yet (step 4 of T48). `release-verify` of `spec` v0.18.3 takes a signature of the shared `release.yml` at v0.1.30: its lower bound is v0.1.7.
- **What is changed here:**
  - `.github/workflows/` — the seven references, from `fab102d # v0.1.24` to `8311cbf # v0.1.30`, in `ci.yml`, `codeql.yml`, `release.yml` and `scorecard.yml`;
  - `ci.yml`, the job "Binaries / build" — the steps «Toolchain image from the cache», «Toolchain image», «Toolchain image into the cache», «Go caches from the cache» and «Go caches» of the job `arm64` of `go.yml` v0.1.30, line for line but for their comments and the check of the key, `binaries` for `arm64`, and at the end of the job «Go caches into the cache» as there; «Release files and samples» runs `novatrail-toolchain` with the folder of the Go caches mounted and the three variables pointed into it, as `go.yml` runs a recipe. The step «Cache steps as in the shared go.yml» takes each of the six steps of the job `arm64` by its name, to the next step or the end of the job, without comments and blank lines, puts `binaries` for `arm64` in the key of the Go caches, and fails the job, naming the step, if one is not there or is not the same lines in "Binaries / build"; and it fails the job if a line of the step `ci-test` of `arm64` that names `/go-cache` — the variables and the mount — is not a line of «Release files and samples»;
  - [.claude/rules/ci.md](../.claude/rules/ci.md) and [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.1 — the version, the one entry of the image, the Go caches, CodeQL's Go.
- **Not changed:** `devcontainer.yml` builds the stage `devcontainer` from the network, as R58 says; the recipes, `release-files` and the binaries — `SHA256SUMS` of `release-files v0.0.0-ci` with a cache of Go filled by a build before it is the same as with an empty one, `e9b0a52b8d56…` (measurements 3.3).

— *Why:* row 7.18 of `COORDINATION.md` spec is the move to the release of `.github` of row 7.13, "for the cache": the time of "Go / test" and of the builds goes to compiling with an empty `GOCACHE` (measurements 3.1 spec). "Binaries / build" compiles six targets, and the cache of Go is what row 7.13 gives a job, so the job takes it, and with it the image from the one entry that every job of `go.yml` and `documents.yml` now reads. The first job of `go.yml`, whose steps R58 copied, now only looks the entry up and hands the image on by an artifact, which a job of the caller cannot wait for: two called workflows, and a job of the caller, see each other's jobs only through the caller's `needs` (R42 .github), and `needs: go` would hold "Binaries" until the last check of `go.yml`. The job `arm64` takes the image on its own, and its steps are the ones a job alone needs. A key of its own: the builds of six targets with `-trimpath` are not those of any check of `go.yml`.

— *Rejected:*
- the steps of the first job of `go.yml`, as T48 said — `lookup-only: true` gives the job no image, and the upload of an artifact is for jobs that `needs: toolchain`, which this one cannot;
- the steps of the job `check` without its step of the artifact — not one run of lines to hold to `go.yml`, and the step names `needs.toolchain`;
- `needs: go` and the artifact `go-toolchain-image` — "Binaries" would wait for every check of `go.yml`, and the artifact is an inner detail of `go.yml`, not its interface;
- "Binaries / build" without the Go caches — the six builds compile with an empty `GOCACHE` on every run, the time row 7.18 is about;
- the key of a check of `go.yml`, as `test` — another job's builds, and two saves of one key on `main`;
- holding to `go.yml` only the three steps of the image, as R58 — the step «Go caches» runs `go mod verify`, and a fix of it in `.github` would not reach this copy;
- waiting for T66 `.github` — it needs the tag of `spec` of row 7.14, which changes `.devcontainer/`, and the coordinator of `spec` keeps `.devcontainer/` unchanged at night (T171 spec): T49 takes it.

— *Consequence:* the 2026-11-16 amendments to R06, R07, R18, R41, R58 and R59. On `main` both "Binaries / build" and the job `toolchain` of `go.yml` may save the one entry of the image on a miss, as `documents.yml` and `go.yml` do, and one save only warns (R42 .github). The entries under the path `toolchain.tar` of v0.1.24 are read no more. The first push to `main` saves the Go caches, and from then on a pull request reads them. The merge changes `release.yml`, which is in the list `paths` (R18), and so gives the next release of the line 0.3, of the same code, signed by the shared `release.yml` v0.1.30. What T66 `.github` gives — the stage `toolchain-core` in the release and the checks, with the tag of `spec` of row 7.14 — T49 takes, with the move to that tag, its `uses:` and, if T66 changes the path of the image, the steps of "Binaries / build", which the step «Cache steps as in the shared go.yml» then requires. — `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `codeql.yml`, `release.yml`, `scorecard.yml`; `.claude/rules/ci.md`; RUN.md, T49; [plan/E4.md](plan/E4.md); [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.1; [sources.md](sources.md), record 3.13; [measurements.md](measurements.md), section 3.3. — RUN.md, T48.

## The tag of `spec` v0.18.4 and the shared checks of `.github` v0.1.31

**R62. `module-kit` is on `spec` v0.18.4, the tag of row 7.14 of `COORDINATION.md` spec: the submodule `spec/` is at this tag, commit 5867a6c, and the Go modules `spec` and `spec/contracts` are v0.18.4 too, the tag of the nested module is `contracts/v0.18.4` at the same commit. `.devcontainer/Dockerfile` has the template's three stages, `toolchain-core`, `toolchain` and `devcontainer`, and CI calls the shared checks of `.github` at v0.1.31, the release of row 7.13 with T66 `.github`, by the SHA of its tag, 93c848f, in every `uses:` of `.github/workflows/`: the release builds its files in `toolchain-core`, and every check runs in `toolchain`, which here is the core with its `SHELL` alone. "Binaries / build" builds in `toolchain`, as the job `arm64` of the shared `go.yml` does, and a step before it fails the job once `toolchain` holds more than the core. The module format is the same 0.4, and the code does not change: the library passes all the vectors of the tag without skips. What the checks prove is the same.** T49 of epic E4, its last task; rows 7.13, 7.14 and 7.18 of `COORDINATION.md` spec, T166 spec, the 2026-11-16 amendments to R31 spec and R40 spec, the sixth 2026-11-15 amendment to R76 spec and R156 spec; the author's word of 2026-11-16 on the change of `.devcontainer/` (T171 spec).
- **What the tag brings** (`git -C spec log --oneline v0.18.3..v0.18.4`, 4 commits, read 2026-11-16) — T166 spec: the stage `toolchain-core` in `spec`'s Dockerfile and the template's — Node, just, Go and buf, what a release needs and every check without a browser —, under `FROM toolchain-core AS toolchain`, to which the kind `go` adds no lines; `toolchain.sh build` takes the stage from `TOOLCHAIN_STAGE` (the 2026-11-16 amendment to R31 spec); `apt` from the snapshot alone (the 2026-11-16 amendment to R40 spec), which this Dockerfile does not run: the core of the kind `go` installs nothing by `apt`; the tests of `tools/` in parts, in `spec`'s own CI (the 2026-11-16 amendment to R48 spec). `format/`, the card architecture 3.7 spec, RULES.md spec and `run/DEBT.md` spec are as at v0.18.3, and no row of table 4 of `spec` that names `module-kit` changed.
